# 3D Reconstruction Daily Papers

The project automatically fetches the latest arXiv papers for 3D reconstruction and 3D Gaussian Splatting keywords.

The subheadings represent the search keywords. For each keyword, the table keeps the most recently updated arXiv records returned by the API.

Click the repository's Watch button to receive notifications from the daily issue workflow.

Last update: 2026-11-19

## 3D reconstruction
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[Refin3R: Geometry-Guided Refinement for Feed-Forward 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23051v1)** | 2026-11-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Feed-forward 3D reconstruction models predict dense point maps from multiple images in a single pass. Without explicit geometric constraints, however, their predictions are often inconsistent across views and lack fine-grained precision. Recent methods incorporate geometric priors such as camera pose or depth, but use them merely as auxiliary inputs, process each view independently, or refine the geometry only where the priors are available. We present Refin3R, which refines feed-forward point maps with Structure-from-Motion (SfM) points, propagating SfM geometry over the image grid. In its dual-branch architecture, a 2D branch extends SfM geometry beyond its coverage using geometry-aware backbone features, while a 3D branch reasons over the SfM points of all views, capturing multi-view structure that the image grid alone cannot represent. The two branches exchange information bidirectionally to jointly refine each view, yielding dense and accurate point maps. Across ScanNet++, ETH3D, Tanks\&amp;Temples, and out-of-domain human and surgical scenes, Refin3R consistently outperforms feed-forward and geometry-guided methods in point map accuracy while running faster than the strongest geometry-guided baseline.</p></details> |  |
| **[SpatialMind: Adaptive Multi-Level Geometric Fusion for Spatial Reasoning in Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22415v1)** | 2026-11-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) demonstrate promising capabilities in 2D visual understanding, yet remain limited in 3D spatial reasoning. Recent studies explore integrating 3D reconstruction models into MLLMs. However, these approaches either use a single geometric granularity across all tasks or adopt a fixed multi-level fusion scheme, regardless of task-specific reasoning requirements. Through systematic experiments on multi-level geometric fusion, we find that spatial tasks at different cognitive levels (e.g., distance estimation vs. route planning) require geometric representations of different granularities, revealing that fixed fusion cannot accommodate diverse spatial reasoning requirements. To bridge this gap, we propose SpatialMind, a task-adaptive framework for dynamically integrating multi-level geometric representations. SpatialMind employs GeoFus, a multi-layer geometric fusion module built around a gating network jointly conditioned on textual instructions, visual semantics, and geometric representations. This network adaptively modulates the contributions of shallow, intermediate, and deep geometric features across the layers of the LLM decoder. Furthermore, we introduce a negative-entropy regularization loss to mitigate gate collapse. Gate visualizations indicate that GeoFus adapts geometric granularity to task requirements. Experiments demonstrate that SpatialMind achieves competitive performance across spatial benchmarks, attaining a state-of-the-art score of 72.3 on VSI-Bench. The source code is publicly available at https://github.com/zhaosb-star/SpatialMind.</p></details> | Includes supplementary material. Code available at https://github.com/zhaosb-star/SpatialMind |
| **[Matisse: Evidence-Space Reasoning for Active 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38746v2)** | 2026-11-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>How can a 3D reconstruction system acquire and retain useful information to understand the geometry of a scene from partial views under a limited computation budget? Existing active view acquisition methods typically estimate uncertainty over observed or instantiated geometry, limiting their ability to reason about unseen structure, while long-horizon reconstruction methods often retain redundant observations. We introduce Matisse, a training-free framework that unifies active reconstruction and keyframe selection by leveraging evidence provided by a pretrained generative 3D model. Matisse estimates Evidential Uncertainty from cross-attention evidence associated with 3D latent tokens and derives an Evidential Information Gain to guide both view acquisition and keyframe selection based on the expected reduction in posterior entropy. Matisse supports multi-object scenes through occlusion-aware, object-balanced aggregation and propagates uncertainty through intermediate latents to avoid full reconstruction during planning. Matisse reduces Chamfer distance by 12.7%, 3.8%, and 9.2% on GSO30, YCB-V, and Replica, respectively, relative to the best baseline on each dataset, and achieves a $1.50\times$ end-to-end speedup over the best active reconstruction baseline on GSO30 with the same backend. In the GSO30 keyframe selection experiment for long-horizon reconstruction, Matisse achieves 18.3% lower Chamfer distance than Stream3D and approaches convergence using roughly 20% as many views.</p></details> | 23 pages, 9 figures, 7 tables. Project page: https://xihangyu630.github.io/matisse/ |
| **[Déjà-Events: Multi-view 3D Geometry with Event Cameras via Sparsity-Aware Adaptive Gating](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21302v1)** | 2026-11-16 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Event cameras keep capturing structure where conventional cameras blur or saturate, which makes them attractive for 3D perception in fast or poorly lit scenes. Event datasets with dense 3D ground truth, however, are far smaller and less diverse than the image collections behind feed-forward 3D reconstruction models. We present Déjà-Events, an event-only model that predicts depth, camera pose and pointmaps from multiple views in a single forward pass. Rather than relying on scarce 3D labels, we distil it from Déjà View, a feed-forward RGB model that refines its tokens by repeatedly applying one shared transformer block. On paired RGB and event recordings, the event student learns to reproduce the frozen teacher's final tokens, which requires no depth or pose ground truth and trains only about 1% of its parameters. Because events are dense at edges and moving texture but nearly absent in textureless regions, we further introduce sparsity-aware adaptive gating, which adapts the shared block's gates to events and adjusts them per patch by event density, so that accuracy holds as refinement steps are added. Trained only on simulated data, Déjà-Events achieves the lowest pointmap error among the event-based methods we evaluate, on EventScape and zero-shot on the real MVSEC and DSEC datasets. On DSEC it halves the error of the strongest baseline (0.15 vs. 0.30 AbsRel) with about a tenth of its parameters. It also has the lowest depth error on EventScape and DSEC and the lowest trajectory error on EventScape.</p></details> | 8 pages, 6 figures |
| **[TetraCoder: Adaptive Tetrahedral Encoding for Neural Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18267v2)** | 2026-11-16 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Implicit and explicit representations of geometry are central in computer graphics applications and come with their pros and cons. With surface sampling being faster on explicit representations and spatial queries being faster on implicit representations, finding efficient conversions between them is a highly desirable property. We present TetraCoder, a single resolution hash-free adaptive tetrahedral encoding that links an explicit tetrahedral spatial encoder with an underlying implicit signed distance field (SDF). The resulting hybrid implicit-explicit space is continuous piecewise affine (CPWA) by design, allowing to obtain an exact correspondence between the implicit SDF and the explicit analytic mesh. TetraCoder's design comes with additional perks, in particular: (a) it scales better than multi-resolution encoding methods due to its single resolution optimizable grid, (b) unlike hash-based methods, it is not affected by collisions, (c) it enables fast tetrahedral ray-tracing, geometry and appearance editing, directly through its adaptive tetrahedral scaffold. We demonstrate the versatility of TetraCoder by showing an improvement over state-of-the-art methods on multiple tasks: 2D/3D fitting from images and point clouds, 3D novel view synthesis, 3D reconstruction, and geometry/appearance editing.</p></details> | 8 pages, 4 figures |
| **[Eco3R: Pinned-Key Inference for Efficient and Streamable Feed-Forward 3D Reconstruction from Long Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18099v2)** | 2026-11-16 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Feed-forward multi-view transformers such as VGGT recover the cameras and geometry of a video in one pass, but the cost of their cross-frame self-attention grows quadratically with the frame count, and their accuracy drops on long videos. Most remedies approximate attention over all frames under a fixed computational budget or select a few keys by their content. We hypothesize that the keys of a long video can be selected independently of video content, and our experiments on VGGT and $π^3$ support it: a small number of uniformly sampled frames can serve as the keys of every frame. We present Eco3R, a training-free inference framework whose keys form a Pinned Key Set of 31 such frames. A pin pass stores their tokens in a Scene Cache, and the other frames read it in independent batches that reproduce joint inference with these keys exactly, at a constant cost per frame. An Online Stream updates the pinned frames during recording. Six middle global blocks align the frames and form a reusable VGGT module: the Alignment Score reads their attention to flag failures, and Alignment Refocusing reduces them by fine-tuning only these blocks (3.6 GPU hours). Eco3R ($π^3$) matches $π^3$'s success rate on 1,000-frame ScanNet videos while running 13.8 times faster. With refocusing, Eco3R (VGGT) raises VGGT's success rate from 70% to 82% while running 8.5 times faster on 500 frames. Eco3R can reconstruct sequences of up to 4,000 frames on a single A100 GPU, while Eco3R ($π^3$) streams 1,000 frames at 18.6 fps, achieving the lowest online absolute trajectory error.</p></details> | 26 pages, 10 figures. v2: renamed the method from EcoVGGT to Eco3R, added Figure 1, and revised the text |
| **[VA-Assembly: View-Aligned Score Guidance for Training-Free 3D Part Assembly](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20788v1)** | 2026-11-16 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Image-to-3D foundation models have improved 3D reconstruction, but typically produce monolithic representations without explicit part-level structure. Part-aware generation and learning-based assembly methods often require part-level supervision, category-specific priors, or paired part-layout training data. We instead consider a practical but underexplored setting in which independently prepared or generated 3D parts have no predefined inter-part relationships, shared coordinate frames, or consistent scales, and must be assembled into a reference-consistent object from a single image. We present VA-Assembly, a training-free framework for 3D part assembly through part-level transformation estimation using view-aligned diffusion guidance. Given a reference image and fixed 3D part geometries, VA-Assembly optimizes each part's translation, rotation, and scale. We condition a frozen view-conditioned diffusion model on same-view renderings of a reference-derived monolithic 3D representation, producing score gradients geometrically aligned with the part configuration. This guidance provides multi-view structural cues under large initial misalignment. By repurposing pretrained visual-structural priors as transformation-level supervision, VA-Assembly requires no annotated part layouts, predefined part taxonomies, category-specific assembly training, or bounding-box-based layout initialization. Experiments compare VA-Assembly with geometric registration and compositional layout baselines using each baseline's supported transformation components, showing improved part-wise transformation estimation. Ablation studies show that view-aligned conditioning, rather than diffusion guidance alone, is critical for reliable transformation gradients. Updating only part transformations preserves explicit component separability and supports component-level editing, replacement, and reconfiguration.</p></details> | 16 pages, 9 figures |
| **[Efficient Multi-View Generation with Iterative Global-Local Proxy Attention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20703v1)** | 2026-11-16 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Generating consistent multi-view RGB images and normal maps from a single input image provides complementary appearance and geometric cues for 3D reconstruction. However, maintaining cross-view consistency requires information exchange across viewpoints, and dense cross-view attention becomes computationally expensive as the number of views and spatial resolution increase. In this paper, we propose Global-Local Proxy Attention (GLPA) to reduce cross-view communication costs while preserving fine-grained within-view processing. Specifically, GLPA retains full-resolution interactions within each view and uses compact global proxies for cross-view communication. To further leverage the exchanged information, GLPA incorporates iterative proxy refinement, which rebuilds the proxies from updated features and reuses the same attention parameters for subsequent interactions within a single denoising step. We evaluate GLPA on two structurally different diffusion backbones, FLUX and Stable Diffusion. Experiments on FLUX show that GLPA achieves a $2.65\times$ speedup in attention computation per denoising step over dense attention, while improving reconstruction IoU from 0.6849 to 0.7019 and reducing NVS LPIPS from 0.086 to 0.076. Results on Stable Diffusion further demonstrate the effectiveness of GLPA, with a $1.80\times$ attention speedup and improvements across all five quality metrics.</p></details> | 10 pages, 6 figures, 5 tables. Supplementary material is available as an ancillary file |
| **[SiGMA: Site-Grounded Multi-Modal ISAC Data Generation for Environment-Aware Communications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20665v1)** | 2026-11-16 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Learning-based multi-modal integrated sensing and communication (ISAC) requires data that associate environmental observations with the wireless response generated by the same site and dynamic scene state. Acquiring such data at new sites requires sensor calibration, coordinated measurements, and cross-modal association. We present site-grounded multi-modal ISAC data generation (SiGMA), which combines OpenStreetMap (OSM), Overture Maps, and procedural three-dimensional (3D) reconstruction to create georeferenced urban scenes. A shared scene geometry and time-indexed actor state link synchronized red-green-blue (RGB) images, light detection and ranging (LiDAR) point clouds, positioning records, frequency-modulated continuous-wave (FMCW) Radar in-phase and quadrature (IQ) samples, and ray-traced wireless channels. For configurable reflected propagation under direct-link blockage, SiGMA generates optional reconfigurable intelligent surface (RIS) component channels for phase-configuration evaluation across shared scene states. The evaluation results demonstrate that the framework improves zero-shot beam prediction performance on real DeepSense 6G measurements compared with an OSM-derived synthetic-data baseline. A camera-assisted RIS case study further demonstrates the generation of sensing-associated supervision for configurable reflected propagation. The dataset generation pipeline is available at https://github.com/Trans-cending/SiGMA-main.</p></details> |  |
| **[Long-Term Multi-Session 3D Reconstruction Under Substantial Appearance Change](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.20584v2)** | 2026-11-16 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Long-term environmental monitoring requires the ability to reconstruct and align 3D models across repeated site visits separated by months or years to enable the detection of structural change and the assessment of ecosystem health. However, existing Structure-from-Motion (SfM) pipelines are generally designed for imagery captured within a limited time window and consequently fail in long-term monitoring scenarios such as repeated coral reef surveys with substantial visual and structural change. In this paper, we show that the primary limitation of current approaches lies in their inability to maintain visual correspondences under substantial temporal appearance change. We address this limitation by enforcing cross-session correspondences within a joint SfM reconstruction. Our approach combines handcrafted and learned visual features to establish correspondences across large temporal gaps, enabling the reconstruction of a single coherent 3D model from imagery captured years apart, where standard SfM fails to connect sessions. To ensure scalability and further improve alignment robustness on large datasets, we restrict expensive learned feature matching to a small set of likely cross-session image pairs identified via visual place recognition, which reduces computational cost while improving the reliability of cross-session correspondence establishment. We evaluate our method on long-term underwater datasets exhibiting significant real-world change, and demonstrate consistent joint reconstruction across sessions in cases where existing methods fail to connect the sessions or align them only coarsely.</p></details> |  |
| **[Pointillist Maize: 3D Reconstruction of Field-Grown Maize Using RGB UAV Imagery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20502v1)** | 2026-11-16 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Three-dimensional reconstruction can extend field phenotyping to individual maize organs, but thin, flexible, occluding structures and plant movement complicate reconstruction. We evaluated whether consumer-grade UAV RGB video processed with Structure from Motion and multi-view stereo could recover recognizable field-grown maize structures, and examined the effects of acquisition trajectories and frame sampling. COLMAP reconstructions preserved recognizable leaves, stems, tassel branches, ears, silks, and brace roots. For an equal-frame comparison, we reconstructed one plant from two sets of 295 frames from the same video: a single orbit sampled at 5 frames/second and three orbits at different altitudes (ribbon skirt) sampled at 2 frames/second. After ground removal, the reconstructions contained 520,601 and 375,318 points, respectively. The single orbit yielded greater point concentration within plant structures and fewer visible gaps, while the ribbon skirt yielded fewer scattered points and slightly lower proportions of isolated points. In densely planted nursery rows, visible voids occupied 17.57% of the rendered scene with one orbit at 1 frame/second, decreasing to 5.07% with additional serpentine passes and 2.68% when the original orbit was sampled at 3 frames/second. Additional observations also increased point dispersion associated with plant movement. A curlicue trajectory tested in a different field in 2024 produced distinguishable individual plants without visible duplicated structures, although substantial voids remained, mainly on the soil surface. These results demonstrate the feasibility of recovering recognizable maize organs from consumer UAV imagery and reveal trade-offs between acquisition strategies, reconstruction completeness, and point dispersion. Independent geometric validation is still needed before quantitative phenotyping.</p></details> | 26 pages, 8 figures, submitted to Frontiers in Plant Science |
| **[SGR3 Model: Scene Graph Retrieval-Reasoning Model in 3D](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.04614v2)** | 2026-11-15 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>3D scene graphs provide a structured representation of object entities and their relationships, enabling high-level interpretation and reasoning for robots while remaining intuitively understandable to humans. Existing approaches for 3D scene graph generation typically combine scene reconstruction with graph neural networks (GNNs). However, such pipelines require multi-modal data that may not always be available, and their reliance on heuristic graph construction can constrain the prediction of relationship triplets. In this work, we introduce a Scene Graph Retrieval-Reasoning Model in 3D (SGR3 Model), a training-free framework that leverages multi-modal large language models (MLLMs) with retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) for semantic scene graph generation. SGR3 Model bypasses the need for explicit 3D reconstruction. Instead, it enhances relational reasoning by incorporating semantically aligned scene graphs retrieved via a ColPali-style cross-modal framework. To improve retrieval robustness, we further introduce a weighted patch-level similarity selection mechanism that mitigates the negative impact of blurry or semantically uninformative regions. Experiments demonstrate that SGR3 Model achieves competitive performance compared to training-free baselines and on par with GNN-based expert models. Moreover, an ablation study on the retrieval module and knowledge base scale reveals that retrieved external information is explicitly integrated into the token generation process, rather than being implicitly internalized through abstraction.</p></details> | There is some problem with the metrics overall |
| **[acuSimNet: Multi-View Self-Occlusion-Awared Visibility Learning for Cranio-Cervical Acupuncture Points](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20025v1)** | 2026-11-15 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The localization of acupuncture points (acupoints) in Traditional Chinese Medicine (TCM) presents unique challenges since they are defined by abstract principles rather than distinct anatomical landmarks. Existing approaches are typically constrained to single view or rely on indirect calculations of relative positions with respect to other landmarks, thereby overlooking human anatomical variations. Furthermore, acupoint visibility assessment, which determins whether points are occluded by human itself, hair, clothing, or other objects, has received limited attention in practical applications. Acupoint localization itself does not require 3D reconstruction, but inferring their occlusion relationships with anatomical surfaces does, which adds computational cost and limits real-time inference. In this work, we introduce acuSimNet, an efficient hierarchical multi-task learning architecture for multi-view, self-occlusion-aware visibility prediction of acupoints, achieving 99.97% accuracy on the validation set. Our approach also addresses the challenges of high-dimensional classification (174 acupoints in cervicocranial region), negative convergence issues for visible acupoints, and inter-task scheduling optimization, resulting in substantially accelerated convergence. We improved the training efficiency from the exisiting methods of 3000 epochs to achieve 99% validation accuracy, to our optimized framework achieving 90% accuracy in 39 epochs and 99% accuracy in 86 epochs. This architecture overcomes the limitations of existing methods, could enable practical applications in acupoints detection and visualization, advancing the automation of TCM.</p></details> | 19 pages, 6 figures, 3 tables |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v3)** | 2026-11-15 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extracting binary contours and their containment hierarchy is a fundamental step in many computer vision pipelines. While the standard Suzuki--Abe algorithm is inherently sequential, existing parallel tracers neither preserve its exact contours nor compute their hierarchy. This paper presents TRUCO (Threaded Raster Unrestricted Contour Ownership), a lock-free parallel algorithm for multi-core CPUs that produces the exact output of Suzuki--Abe. TRUCO relies on two core ideas. First, the image is partitioned into horizontal stripes, and a geometric ownership rule allows each thread to trace complete contours independently across stripe boundaries, avoiding both locks and post-processing merges. Second, contour tracing is decoupled from hierarchy reconstruction, which is performed in a subsequent parallel pass and can be skipped entirely when only the contours are needed. We formally prove that TRUCO is deterministic, data-race-free, and identical in output to Suzuki--Abe regardless of thread count. Across diverse high-resolution datasets-from sparse segmentation masks to dense real-world photographs-TRUCO achieves speedups of 4.7x to 17.4x over the fastest sequential baseline with hierarchy computation, and 2.3x to 17.8x without it.</p></details> |  |
| **[Separating perception from reasoning in vision-language models: a model-free render ceiling for crystal structures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.00663v2)** | 2026-11-14 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Vision-language models (VLMs) often predict plausible structural labels from images without recovering the three-dimensional geometry behind them. End-to-end scores cannot tell whether an error comes from ambiguous views, lost visual information, or flawed reasoning. Multi-view 3D reconstruction faces its own trade-off: rigid symmetry priors erase genuine distortions, while unconstrained triangulation absorbs measurement outliers. We separate visual perception from structural reasoning through an inspectable 3D geometric intermediate. Its foundation is a model-free render ceiling: an exact reference for what calibrated, species-labeled projections determine, obtained by ruling out projective phantoms. Across a 1,950-structure census, every tested combination of four or five calibrated views uniquely determined the 3D sites from complete, species-labeled centroids. Against this reference, replacing images with exact text coordinates lowers the error rate of all 17 evaluated VLMs on crystals. The three frontier models gain 10.0-19.5 percentage points yet still miss 8.1-21.0% of cases. To recover geometry from imperfect observations, our symmetry-aware split reconstruction jointly estimates a symmetric base, coherent 3D physical displacements, and view-specific 2D corruption, adaptively selecting between atomic and collective distortion modes. Relative to Huber triangulation, it reduces coordinate error by 36.7% under Gaussian noise and 41.2% under corruption. Adaptive mode selection further cuts collective error up to 30.4% relative to atomic split across two external cohorts without sacrificing localized fidelity. Given reconstructed rather than Huber coordinates, downstream models improve. Geometric recovery thus becomes an explicit, testable foundation for structural understanding.</p></details> |  |
| **[AgriChrono: A Longitudinal Dataset and Benchmark for Novel-View Synthesis of Field-Grown Crops](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.18694v4)** | 2026-11-14 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Reliable 3D reconstruction of field-grown crops is hindered by thin, overlapping structures, wind-driven foliage motion, and changing illumination. Yet longitudinal field datasets and standardized novel-view synthesis (NVS) benchmarks remain scarce. We present an integrated framework for robotic acquisition, dataset construction, NVS evaluation, and agricultural scene reconstruction. AgriChrobot enables web-based teleoperation and repeated multi-view capture along crop rows. Using this platform, we introduce AgriChrono, a longitudinal canola dataset collected over approximately one month, comprising 160 recording sessions, four RGB streams, two depth maps, and estimated camera poses. We define seven NVS scenarios spanning four times of day and three acquisition dates, and benchmark 15 NeRF- and Gaussian Splatting-based methods. In addition to whole-image metrics, we assess fine-detail reconstruction within manually annotated canola regions using gradient- and edge-based metrics. Our results show that strong whole-image scores do not guarantee faithful reconstruction of thin, overlapping crop structures. To address this gap, we propose AgriSplat, which relaxes visibility filtering during initialization and reallocates model capacity through contribution-based pruning and budgeted reinvestment in sub-threshold candidates ranked by existing gradient statistics. After reallocation, asymmetric directional supervision penalizes insufficient contrast along reference image-gradient directions. Relative to Octree-GS, AgriSplat improves mean crop-region gradient-energy ratio from 0.506 to 0.847 and edge F-score from 0.712 to 0.797 across seven scenarios, while also improving whole-image PSNR, SSIM, and LPIPS. These contributions provide a dataset, benchmark, and method for systematic crop reconstruction under field conditions.</p></details> | Keywords: Agricultural robotics, Multimodal dataset, Longitudinal crop monitoring, Illumination variability, 3D Gaussian Splatting, Novel view synthesis, 3D Reconstruction |
| **[Mamba3R: Fixed-Capacity Selective State Memory for Long-Sequence 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04221v2)** | 2026-11-13 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Streaming 3D reconstruction must maintain geometric quality over long inputs within a fixed GPU budget. A pretrained recurrent model provides bounded state, but freezing its backbone also fixes its learned state-observation interactions. We introduce Mamba3R, a jointly designed state-memory adapter that provides a trainable adaptation path without updating the backbone or task heads. A selective state-space mixer jointly transforms native state features and a fixed-capacity auxiliary memory, explicit read and write paths carry information across frames, and a decomposed feedback path regulates what the added memory feeds back. Only 1.28 million adapter parameters are trained. Under a shared periodic-reset protocol, it lowers trajectory error and improves accumulated surface and depth accuracy over recurrent baselines on public indoor and outdoor datasets and robot-recorded traversals, gaining up to 40 percent on one metric. Matched comparisons support both the choice of the mixing operator and the utility of carrying the auxiliary memory across frames. Allocated memory remains unchanged and throughput moves by at most 1.4 percent from one to two thousand frames. Replaying its recorded traversals offline, the platform's RTX 4060 averages 11.7 frames per second within 5.1 GiB with the same checkpoint, a mean throughput covering the 10 Hz sensor rate.</p></details> | 12 pages, 4 figures, 8 tables. The supplementary material referenced in the text is not included in this version |
| **[TelePhysica: Agentic Reconstruction of Physically Interactive Scenes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18078v1)** | 2026-11-13 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Direct image-to-video diffusion models entangle pixel synthesis with implicit latent motion, precluding persistent 3D mechanical equilibrium, mid-rollout intervention, and counterfactual force control. Conversely, single-pass programmatic 3D reconstruction from an uncalibrated photograph leaves monocular depth and support clearance underconstrained, causing reconstructed scenes to interpenetrate or collapse under gravity. We present TelePhysica, a closed-loop framework that reconstructs an executable, multi-regime physical world from a single photograph to drive stateful 3D interaction and trajectory-grounded video synthesis. A vision-language coding agent synthesizes separable visual meshes and declarative physical models within a two-stage verification loop that pairs pixel-aligned silhouette critique with a zero-force gravitational equilibrium preflight. The verified world compiles into a unified solver coupling rigid bodies, articulated chains, co-dimensional cloth shells, and 3D tetrahedral elastodynamics, whose simulated rollouts condition pretrained video diffusion models through pixel-aligned structural streams. On a 100-scene single-image benchmark, closed-loop execution raises equilibrium-verified world recovery from $0\%$ in single-pass synthesis to $81\%$. Controlled constitutive ablations show over an order-of-magnitude larger localized corner displacement and out-of-plane pleating in finite-element cloth than in matched rigid-plate proxies. Conditioning video diffusion on simulated trajectories increases dynamic motion-mask overlap by $2{\times}\text{--}5{\times}$ and preserves motion direction under counterfactual force reversals where direct image-to-video models fail.</p></details> | Ongoing Work. Homepage: https://interactive4d.vercel.app/ |
| **[Real-VAS: a Real-World Video Amodal Segmentation Dataset](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00430v2)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Amodal video object segmentation is fundamentally limited by the absence of datasets that combine real-world complexity with precise ground-truth annotations. To address this, we present Real Video Amodal Segmentation (Real-VAS), a new large-scale, zero-shot evaluation dataset built from composited real-image video. We introduce a novel data generation pipeline that enables the creation of programmatically consistent amodal ground truth for real-world footage, in which the object mask, occlusion boundary, and composited image are all derived from a single unoccluded source, without relying on human estimation or expensive 3D reconstruction. Our dataset is structured into two challenging scenarios: dynamic Occlusion, created by compositing two clips of moving objects, and a unique Container category featuring complex, physically constrained interactions. These container scenarios--On Surface Containment, Articulated Containment, and Mobile Containment--allow us to generate precise ground truth by simulating an object's motion based on its container's tracked transformation. As a result, Real-VAS provides a diverse and challenging benchmark for evaluating amodal segmentation models on realistic video with the precision of synthetic data. The dataset and generation code are publicly available at https://finlay-hudson.github.io/real_vas</p></details> | 34 pages, including supplementary material. Project page: https://finlay-hudson.github.io/real_vas Code: https://github.com/finlay-hudson/Real-VAS Dataset: https://huggingface.co/datasets/fhudson96/Real-VAS |
| **[BrickFlow: Connectivity-Guided Brick Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17321v1)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Given a set of LEGO parts and an image of an assembled object, our task is to infer the pose of each part to assemble the object. In contrast with traditional 3D reconstruction, doing so requires satisfying discrete physical constraints, which provide structure but render the problem combinatorial and highly sensitive to small pose errors. To learn a prior over how parts fit together, we train a set-conditioned SE(3) flow model on procedurally generated assemblies. We then finetune this model to inject image conditioning, using 1% as much data. However, while the learned flow captures global structure, it does not enforce connectivity. To address this, we introduce an analytic connectivity-field guidance to pull predictions toward connected assemblies at test time. Through this, we demonstrate improved build quality with model size, pretraining, and guidance. We make our models and data available for research purposes at https://kulits.github.io/BrickFlow/</p></details> | NeurIPS 2026 |
| **[GC-MoE on SM120: Overlapping Expert-Parallel Token Dispatch with Warp-Level FP8xFP4 Block-Scaled MMA on Consumer Blackwell](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16219v1)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Expert-parallel Mixture-of-Experts decoding is latency bound: each layer serializes a token exchange, a grouped GEMM over routed experts, and a combine, while only a few tokens per rank are in flight. Prior GC-MoE fused these phases into one persistent, Green-Context-partitioned CUDA Graph on Hopper (SM90) and datacenter Blackwell (SM100). This paper rebuilds the operator for SM120 (compute capability 12.0, e.g. RTX PRO 5000) over PCIe. SM120 exposes block-scaled tensor cores only through warp-level mma.sync.aligned.kind::mxf8f6f4: no tensor memory, no two-CTA MMA, no cluster multicast. | **[SpaceMCP: Vision Language Model Spatial Reasoning via Model Context Protocol](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15680v1)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Despite impressive advances in large language models (LLM), vision-language models (VLM) still exhibit limited spatial reasoning capabilities. Recent progress in feed-forward 3D reconstruction has enabled precise, real-time geometric understanding from visual inputs. In this work, we propose SpaceMCP, a framework that leverages these advances to introduce an MCP-style paradigm for spatial reasoning. Our approach augments a VLM with structured function calls, allowing it to query a scene graph for spatial information such as distances, object positioning, and camera properties. The scene graph is constructed in real time using feed-forward 3D reconstruction to recover metric geometry, combined with open-vocabulary segmentation to identify and localize relevant objects within the scene. Paired with the VLM's strong reasoning capabilities, the VLM is able to combine this primitive information to solve more complex spatial tasks. Without any training, SpaceMCP achieves state-of-the-art results on VSI-Bench, MMSI-Bench and VSTI-Bench, outperforming recent spatially post-trained VLMs and improving its base VLM by 15-22 points per benchmark. We argue that, given the contrast between the relatively slow progress in native VLM spatial reasoning and the rapid advances in feed-forward 3D computer vision, this paradigm offers a promising path forward for enabling robust spatial reasoning.</p></details> | 13 pages, 3 figures. Accepted at the 2nd Workshop on Embodied Spatial Reasoning (ESR) at NeurIPS 2026 |
| **[ThermalSplat: Generalizable Pose-Free Gaussian Splatting for Sparse-View Thermal 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15435v1)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Thermal infrared imaging is vital for applications like search-and-rescue and autonomous navigation, yet trans- lating it into 3D scene representations remains a critical bottleneck. The low-texture nature and view-dependent emissivity of thermal images cause standard pose estima- tors to fail catastrophically, collapsing standard 3D Gaus- sian Splatting pipelines under sparse-view constraints. Fur- thermore, existing thermal reconstruction methods rely heavily on dense capture and computationally expensive per-scene optimization. Critically, many of these ap- proaches depend on aligned RGB-thermal datasets to ob- tain reliable camera poses and initial point clouds, ren- dering them unusable in purely thermal, unposed envi- ronments. To break this bottleneck, we present Thermal- Splat, the first generalizable, feed-forward Gaussian splat- ting framework that reconstructs 3D scenes directly from sparse thermal images without requiring known camera poses, RGB guidance, or per-scene training. Trained on an aggregated multi-source dataset, ThermalSplat synthesizes sharp reconstructions of unseen scenes in a single feed-forward pass. Extensive experi- ments demonstrate that ThermalSplat holds a strict advan- tage over scene-specific thermal splatting methods in the sparse regime. Furthermore, even compared against these baselines operating under their ideal dense capture set- tings, ThermalSplat achieves superior perceptual fidelity. By eliminating the need for computationally expensive per scene optimization, ThermalSplat offers an efficient and generalizable approach to thermal 3D reconstruction.</p></details> | WACV 2027 |
| **[GUSH3R: Everyone Everywhere All at Once as Gaussians](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05243v2)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Reconstructing dynamic human-scene environments from monocular videos is a challenging problem that requires jointly modeling scene geometry, camera motion, and non-rigid human dynamics while enabling photorealistic rendering. From a monocular human-scene video, our method reconstructs dynamic humans (everyone) and static scenes (everywhere) in a single forward pass (all at once) as 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) primitives (as gaussians), which are geometrically consistent and capable of novel view synthesis. Experiments on monocular human-scene datasets demonstrate that our approach achieves competitive novel view synthesis quality while significantly improving inference efficiency compared to optimization-based methods.</p></details> | Project page: https://abkeito.github.io/gush3r-page/ Accepted to WACV 2027 |
| **[Neural Latent Gaussian Process for 3D reconstruction of Mouse Brain Lipids](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18744v1)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We address the problem of reconstructing continuous three-dimensional lipid profiles of the mouse brain from lipid measurements acquired only on a sparse set of two-dimensional tissue sections. We propose a Gaussian Process prior for the distribution of low-dimensional lipid profiles across the mouse brain (with Matérn or a Riemann manifold kernel). This allows us to encode 3D coordinates in a small number of latent 3D volumes that are then mapped to particular lipids using a multilayer perceptron (MLP). We performed an exhaustive analysis over a range of algorithms from simple linear regression, XGBoost, MLP to state of the art models such as Neural Transcriptomic Field, graph-based SPA3D for spatial omics reconstruction and DEEPSPATIAL. Across 173 measured lipids, LGP outperforms all evaluated baselines, including recent spatial-omics models and GPLFR, achieving a mean correlation of 0.613 and demonstrating the effectiveness of our approach for sparse 3D lipid interpolation.</p></details> | 24 pages, 14 figures, 5 tables |

The crossover combines a base solution with multiple donor solutions in a single recombination operation, thereby expanding the space of available edge exchanges. It uses virtual copies of the base solution to establish edge balance, together with an auxiliary directed graph and a budgeted search to construct valid AB-cycles. Extensive experiments on 215 benchmark instances across four sets show that the proposed algorithm improves the best-known solution values for 34 instances and matches those for another 168. | **[MatrixSplat: Matrix-Parallel and Feature-Scalable 3D Gaussian Splatting Rendering on GPUs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15153v1)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has emerged as a mainstream representation for real-time novel view synthesis and 3D reconstruction. However, existing 3DGS rendering pipelines are poorly aligned with modern GPU architectures: depth-sorted alpha compositing introduces strong per-pixel sequential dependencies, limiting parallelism and resulting in scalar FMA-dominated execution that underutilizes high-throughput matrix units such as Tensor Cores. This mismatch becomes more severe for multimodal and semantically enriched 3DGS, where high-dimensional features must be accumulated from millions of Gaussians, leading to rapidly increasing computation and memory costs. We propose MatrixSplat, a matrix-parallel rendering framework for feature-scalable 3DGS on modern GPUs. We first identify the sequential dependency of alpha compositing as a key obstacle to efficient high-dimensional feature rendering, and adopt an order-independent weighted accumulation formulation that transforms feature rendering into matrix-oriented computation. Our framework features three key designs: (i) a tile-based splatting pipeline that maps feature accumulation to warp-level matrix-multiply-accumulate execution on Tensor Cores; (ii) a warp-granularity Gaussian packing strategy that mitigates visibility irregularity and improves feature reuse; and (iii) a lazy channel-batched feature-fetching pipeline that reduces shared-memory pressure, maintains high warp occupancy, and overlaps memory access with matrix execution for high-dimensional features. On Mip-NeRF 360, LERF, and 3D-OVS, MatrixSplat achieves up to 3.8$\times$ speedup over 3DGS and 2$\times$ over TC-GS while maintaining rendering quality and semantic accuracy, and further reduces render-kernel time by up to 36.7$\times$ for high-dimensional features. | **[RIGOR: Rig-Informed Geometry for Omnidirectional Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13504v3)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Recent developments in feed-forward 3D reconstruction resulted in models which can recover dense scene representations and camera motion solely from an image stream. Source code is available at https://github.com/Anwarshamim01/multi-view-3d-reconstruction-using-v-jepa-features |

## 三维重建
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v3)** | 2026-11-15 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extracting binary contours and their containment hierarchy is a fundamental step in many computer vision pipelines. While the standard Suzuki--Abe algorithm is inherently sequential, existing parallel tracers neither preserve its exact contours nor compute their hierarchy. This paper presents TRUCO (Threaded Raster Unrestricted Contour Ownership), a lock-free parallel algorithm for multi-core CPUs that produces the exact output of Suzuki--Abe. TRUCO relies on two core ideas. First, the image is partitioned into horizontal stripes, and a geometric ownership rule allows each thread to trace complete contours independently across stripe boundaries, avoiding both locks and post-processing merges. Second, contour tracing is decoupled from hierarchy reconstruction, which is performed in a subsequent parallel pass and can be skipped entirely when only the contours are needed. We formally prove that TRUCO is deterministic, data-race-free, and identical in output to Suzuki--Abe regardless of thread count. Across diverse high-resolution datasets-from sparse segmentation masks to dense real-world photographs-TRUCO achieves speedups of 4.7x to 17.4x over the fastest sequential baseline with hierarchy computation, and 2.3x to 17.8x without it.</p></details> |  |

On 8xRTX PRO 5000 over PCIe the operator is 1.522x and 1.387x faster than the FlashInfer CuTe DSL W4A8 MegaMoE and the Cake MegaMoE across 18 decode points (18/18 wins each), and 1.388x faster than Cake with shared experts fused; all 94 numerical cases and 752 per-rank checks pass, at cross-operator cosine similarity of at least 0.9999997.</p></details> | 24 pages, 10 tables, 6 figures |

After one LoRA RFT epoch (matched WikiText-2 protocol), LILA Act surpasses SliceGPT+RFT by 6.39~pp on average using 98.4\% fewer calibration samples (16 vs.\ 1,024) across LLaMA-2-7B and Phi-2. It uses virtual copies of the base solution to establish edge balance, together with an auxiliary directed graph and a budgeted search to construct valid AB-cycles. Extensive experiments on 215 benchmark instances across four sets show that the proposed algorithm improves the best-known solution values for 34 instances and matches those for another 168. Additional experiments indicate that a moderate number of parents provides beneficial diversity, whereas excessive parent counts, particularly when $p$ is large, lead to excessive population dispersion and hinder convergence.</p></details> |  |
| **[An exact compress-with-another threshold in a binary automaton family](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07044v2)** | 2026-11-10 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>For a state $q$ in a synchronizing automaton, let $μ(q)$ be the minimum length of a word merging $q$ with another initial state. Szykuła asked whether $μ(q)=O(n)$ for strongly connected synchronizing automata and described a binary family attributed to Dżyga, with $n=3p$ states and a conjectured threshold $4n/3$. We specify the boundary transitions omitted from the published piecewise definition and prove that the resulting automata $\mathcal A_p$ satisfy $μ(q_0)=4p=4n/3$ for all $p\geq3$. A shortest word merging $q_0$ with another state is $(ba)^p(ab)^p$. We also construct a reset word of length $3p^2+4p-1$. Consequently, any uniform linear upper bound for $μ(q)$ on binary strongly connected synchronizing automata with corank-one letters has leading constant at least $4/3$.</p></details> | 9 pages, 1 figure. Revised proofs and exposition, corrected boundary transitions, updated references, and added a coauthor |
| **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset comprises 770 RANS simulations of 194 unique wing configurations, sampled across 27 airfoil profile variants and 4 distances from the ground, spanning variations in wing sweep, chord, taper, angle of attack, and tip twist. Six neural network architectures representing distinct paradigms --- convolutional (DoMINO), graph-based (X-MeshGraphNet), transformer-based (AB-UPT, GeoTransolver, GAOT), and JEPA-based (AeroJEPA) --- are evaluated on their ability to predict surface pressure coefficient and wall shear stress distributions, from which integral drag and lift coefficients are recovered. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |
| **[Active Learning for Gaussian Process Regression Under Self-Induced Boltzmann Weights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10654v2)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We consider the active learning problem where the goal is to learn an unknown function with low prediction error under an unknown Boltzmann distribution induced by the function itself. This self-induced weighting arises naturally in problems such as potential energy surface (PES) modeling in computational chemistry, yet poses unique challenges as the target distribution is unknown and its partition function is intractable. We propose \texttt{AB-SID-iVAR}, a Gaussian Process-based acquisition function that approximates the intractable Bayesian target distribution in closed form while avoiding partition function estimation, and is applicable to both discrete and continuous input domains. We also analyze a Thompson sampling alternative (\texttt{TS-SID-iVAR}) as a higher variance Monte Carlo variant. Despite the unknown target, under mild conditions, we establish that the terminal prediction error vanishes with high probability, and provide a tighter average-case guarantee. We demonstrate consistent improvements over existing approaches in this setting on synthetic benchmarks and real-world PES modeling and drug discovery tasks. Our code is available at https://github.com/TsingQAQ/SIDAL</p></details> |  |

## multi-view stereo
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[Pointillist Maize: 3D Reconstruction of Field-Grown Maize Using RGB UAV Imagery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20502v1)** | 2026-11-16 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Three-dimensional reconstruction can extend field phenotyping to individual maize organs, but thin, flexible, occluding structures and plant movement complicate reconstruction. We evaluated whether consumer-grade UAV RGB video processed with Structure from Motion and multi-view stereo could recover recognizable field-grown maize structures, and examined the effects of acquisition trajectories and frame sampling. COLMAP reconstructions preserved recognizable leaves, stems, tassel branches, ears, silks, and brace roots. For an equal-frame comparison, we reconstructed one plant from two sets of 295 frames from the same video: a single orbit sampled at 5 frames/second and three orbits at different altitudes (ribbon skirt) sampled at 2 frames/second. After ground removal, the reconstructions contained 520,601 and 375,318 points, respectively. The single orbit yielded greater point concentration within plant structures and fewer visible gaps, while the ribbon skirt yielded fewer scattered points and slightly lower proportions of isolated points. In densely planted nursery rows, visible voids occupied 17.57% of the rendered scene with one orbit at 1 frame/second, decreasing to 5.07% with additional serpentine passes and 2.68% when the original orbit was sampled at 3 frames/second. Additional observations also increased point dispersion associated with plant movement. A curlicue trajectory tested in a different field in 2024 produced distinguishable individual plants without visible duplicated structures, although substantial voids remained, mainly on the soil surface. These results demonstrate the feasibility of recovering recognizable maize organs from consumer UAV imagery and reveal trade-offs between acquisition strategies, reconstruction completeness, and point dispersion. Independent geometric validation is still needed before quantitative phenotyping.</p></details> | 26 pages, 8 figures, submitted to Frontiers in Plant Science |

| **[SemLite-GS: Semantic-Selective Parallel Gaussian Splatting for Lightweight Building Reconstruction from UAV Oblique Imagery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19773v1)** | 2026-11-15 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Lightweight 3D building models support urban spatial analysis, but their production often requires dense scene reconstruction, building extraction, and mesh simplification. With comparable polygon budgets, SemLite-GS achieves superior preservation of dominant planar structures and sharp architectural transitions compared with conventional dense reconstruction followed by mesh decimation. Project page: https://martine-benchmark.github.io/ |

## structure from motion
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[Refin3R: Geometry-Guided Refinement for Feed-Forward 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23051v1)** | 2026-11-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Feed-forward 3D reconstruction models predict dense point maps from multiple images in a single pass. Without explicit geometric constraints, however, their predictions are often inconsistent across views and lack fine-grained precision. Recent methods incorporate geometric priors such as camera pose or depth, but use them merely as auxiliary inputs, process each view independently, or refine the geometry only where the priors are available. We present Refin3R, which refines feed-forward point maps with Structure-from-Motion (SfM) points, propagating SfM geometry over the image grid. In its dual-branch architecture, a 2D branch extends SfM geometry beyond its coverage using geometry-aware backbone features, while a 3D branch reasons over the SfM points of all views, capturing multi-view structure that the image grid alone cannot represent. The two branches exchange information bidirectionally to jointly refine each view, yielding dense and accurate point maps. Across ScanNet++, ETH3D, Tanks\&amp;Temples, and out-of-domain human and surgical scenes, Refin3R consistently outperforms feed-forward and geometry-guided methods in point map accuracy while running faster than the strongest geometry-guided baseline.</p></details> |  |
| **[Long-Term Multi-Session 3D Reconstruction Under Substantial Appearance Change](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.20584v2)** | 2026-11-16 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Long-term environmental monitoring requires the ability to reconstruct and align 3D models across repeated site visits separated by months or years to enable the detection of structural change and the assessment of ecosystem health. However, existing Structure-from-Motion (SfM) pipelines are generally designed for imagery captured within a limited time window and consequently fail in long-term monitoring scenarios such as repeated coral reef surveys with substantial visual and structural change. In this paper, we show that the primary limitation of current approaches lies in their inability to maintain visual correspondences under substantial temporal appearance change. We address this limitation by enforcing cross-session correspondences within a joint SfM reconstruction. Our approach combines handcrafted and learned visual features to establish correspondences across large temporal gaps, enabling the reconstruction of a single coherent 3D model from imagery captured years apart, where standard SfM fails to connect sessions. To ensure scalability and further improve alignment robustness on large datasets, we restrict expensive learned feature matching to a small set of likely cross-session image pairs identified via visual place recognition, which reduces computational cost while improving the reliability of cross-session correspondence establishment. We evaluate our method on long-term underwater datasets exhibiting significant real-world change, and demonstrate consistent joint reconstruction across sessions in cases where existing methods fail to connect the sessions or align them only coarsely.</p></details> |  |
| **[Pointillist Maize: 3D Reconstruction of Field-Grown Maize Using RGB UAV Imagery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20502v1)** | 2026-11-16 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Three-dimensional reconstruction can extend field phenotyping to individual maize organs, but thin, flexible, occluding structures and plant movement complicate reconstruction. In densely planted nursery rows, visible voids occupied 17.57% of the rendered scene with one orbit at 1 frame/second, decreasing to 5.07% with additional serpentine passes and 2.68% when the original orbit was sampled at 3 frames/second. Additional observations also increased point dispersion associated with plant movement. | **[GC-MoE on SM120: Overlapping Expert-Parallel Token Dispatch with Warp-Level FP8xFP4 Block-Scaled MMA on Consumer Blackwell](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16219v1)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Expert-parallel Mixture-of-Experts decoding is latency bound: each layer serializes a token exchange, a grouped GEMM over routed experts, and a combine, while only a few tokens per rank are in flight. | **[Track-Free Dense Structure from Motion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20858v3)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Classical Bundle Adjustment (BA) relies on precise metric initialization and known camera intrinsics, and traditional Structure-from-Motion (SfM) pipelines struggle to exploit modern dense matchers because rigid track-building heuristics break down under their inherent noise. | **[Budget-aware Correspondence-driven View Selection for Real-time 3D Perception of Mobile Robots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11809v1)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>3D visual perception and scene reconstruction are critical for autonomous robots and intelligent transportation, yet real-world deployments often generate long, redundant image sequences that hinder downstream processing. ## surface reconstruction
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

| **[MedGS: Gaussian Splatting for Multi-Modal 3D Medical Imaging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.16806v5)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multi-modal three-dimensional (3D) medical imaging data, derived from ultrasound, magnetic resonance imaging (MRI), and computed tomography (CT), provide a widely adopted approach for non-invasive anatomical visualization. However, accurate modeling depends on surface reconstruction and frame-to-frame interpolation, where traditional methods often struggle with image noise and incomplete information between sparse frames. To address these challenges, we present \our{}, a novel framework based on Gaussian Splatting (GS) designed for high-fidelity 3D anatomical reconstruction. Uniquely, \our{} employs a multi-task architecture that simultaneously performs frame interpolation and segmentation using a unified geometric representation. By coupling these tasks, the model leverages dense signals from image synthesis to regularize the geometry, enabling high-quality surface extraction even from a limited number of input frames. Specifically, medical data are modeled as Folded-Gaussians with dual color attributes, supported by an In-Between Frame Regularization (IBFR) mechanism. Experimental results demonstrate that \our{} achieves higher metric scores than implicit neural representations and improves interpolation quality. Repository: https://github.com/gmum/MedGS</p></details> |  |

The parametric representation further enables localised modelling of airway stenosis and dilation without re-segmentation. Evaluated on 42 reference airway masks from ATM22 (15 cases) and AeroPath (27 cases), the framework produced 37 topologically accepted reconstructions, with a median port-to-loop distance of 0.123 mm across 2174 matched ports. Reconstructed and reference equivalent radii showed strong agreement ($R^2 = 0.976$), with a mean bias of -0.414 mm arising primarily from the circular, inscribed-radius representation. Controlled pathology modelling generated 40 variants across five patient-specific geometries, including 25-75% stenosis and 50% dilation at tracheal and main-bronchus locations. Prescribed radius changes were reproduced with a mean absolute ratio error of 0.0056, while the median 95th-percentile deviation outside the modified region was 0.111 mm. Downstream applicability was demonstrated through volume meshing and an OpenFOAM simulation with individually prescribed model-based resistance conditions at 62 outlets. Although additional surface preparation may be required for numerical meshing, CT-RespGeomLib provides a reproducible framework for CT-derived airway reconstruction, quantitative boundary validation and controlled geometric pathology modelling.</p></details> |  |

| **[Delaunay-Based Nearest Neighbor Search with Grid-Accelerated Queries and Parallel Construction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15320v1)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Nearest-neighbor search is a fundamental primitive in computer graphics, with applications in distance field computation, surface reconstruction, and point cloud registration. In many graphics applications, data points lie on a surface manifold embedded in 3D space, whereas queries are distributed throughout the surrounding volume, a setting in which spatial partitioning structures such as k-d trees and grids can become inefficient. For construction, we analyze how parallel insertion affects query cost and develop a parallel algorithm that preserves logarithmic expected query cost under stated geometric assumptions. Our parallel construction algorithm achieves a 10x speedup over the sequential baseline while maintaining nearly the same query cost. We further reorder query points to improve cache locality. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method outperforms widely used libraries in nearest-neighbor query performance.</p></details> |  |

It uses virtual copies of the base solution to establish edge balance, together with an auxiliary directed graph and a budgeted search to construct valid AB-cycles. Additional experiments indicate that a moderate number of parents provides beneficial diversity, whereas excessive parent counts, particularly when $p$ is large, lead to excessive population dispersion and hinder convergence.</p></details> |  |

Extensive experiments and analysis demonstrate the effectiveness of our dataset and annotation framework for 3D surgical point tracking, reconstruction, and surgical action understanding. Homepage: https://wrld.github.io/surgtap-3d/</p></details> | Accepted to IEEE Transactions on Robotics (T-RO) |
| **[Martine: Benchmarking Multi-View 3D Surface Reconstruction Across Viewpoint Coverage, Resolution, and Lighting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11602v1)** | 2026-11-08 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Controlled acquisition setups are widely used for high-accuracy multi-view 3D surface reconstruction, as they allow sensor configuration, image resolution, viewpoint distribution, and illumination to be specified. Project page: https://martine-benchmark.github.io/ |

## neural rendering
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[LoC-IN: Real-Time Localization of Cameras in Neural Rendered Prior LiDAR Maps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.16349v2)** | 2026-11-16 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Accurate camera localization is crucial for robotics and Extended Reality (XR), enabling reliable navigation and alignment of virtual and real content. Existing visual methods often suffer from drift, scale ambiguity, and depend on fiducials or loop closure. This work introduces a real-time method for localizing a camera within a pre-captured, highly accurate colored LiDAR point cloud. By rendering synthetic views from this cloud, 2D-3D correspondences are established between live frames and the point cloud. A neural rendering technique narrows the domain gap between synthetic and real images, reducing occlusion and background artifacts to improve feature matching. The result is drift-free camera tracking with correct metric scale in the global LiDAR coordinate system. Two real-time variants are presented: Online Render and Match (R&amp;M), and Prebuild and Localize (P&amp;L). We demonstrate improved results on the ScanNet++ dataset and outperform existing SLAM pipelines.</p></details> | Published in IEEE Robotics and Automation Letters, 8 pages, 4 figures |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v3)** | 2026-11-15 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extracting binary contours and their containment hierarchy is a fundamental step in many computer vision pipelines. While the standard Suzuki--Abe algorithm is inherently sequential, existing parallel tracers neither preserve its exact contours nor compute their hierarchy. This paper presents TRUCO (Threaded Raster Unrestricted Contour Ownership), a lock-free parallel algorithm for multi-core CPUs that produces the exact output of Suzuki--Abe. TRUCO relies on two core ideas. First, the image is partitioned into horizontal stripes, and a geometric ownership rule allows each thread to trace complete contours independently across stripe boundaries, avoiding both locks and post-processing merges. Second, contour tracing is decoupled from hierarchy reconstruction, which is performed in a subsequent parallel pass and can be skipped entirely when only the contours are needed. We formally prove that TRUCO is deterministic, data-race-free, and identical in output to Suzuki--Abe regardless of thread count. Across diverse high-resolution datasets-from sparse segmentation masks to dense real-world photographs-TRUCO achieves speedups of 4.7x to 17.4x over the fastest sequential baseline with hierarchy computation, and 2.3x to 17.8x without it.</p></details> |  |

Even minor adjustments require re-rendering massive scenes with slow offline renderers; global illumination has to be sampled and complex shaders have to be evaluated, leading to iteration times of minutes to hours per frame. To accelerate this process, we introduce a novel neural render proxy (NRP) that enables differentiable relighting of static scenes with fixed camera and materials at interactive rates. Our main insight is the decoupling of traditional rendering into path sampling and emission computation. From a single, light-agnostic render pass, we collect light transport data in the form of path samples. This enables rapidly sampling lighting conditions on the fly, and training a scene-specific lightweight neural network that learns how light is transported from any scene location to any image pixel. This approach is compatible with non-differentiable production renderers, induces minimal memory requirements during inference, and scales only with resolution and the number of light sources and parameters, but independently of scene and appearance complexity. Our evaluation demonstrates interactive frame rates for relighting (~30-60 Hz) while closely approximating the visual fidelity of ground-truth path tracing. In addition, the differentiable NRP enables fast, gradient-based inverse workflows, allowing artists to efficiently solve for lighting parameters from intuitive image-space edits or generative targets.</p></details> | Accepted at Computer Graphics Forum (Proc. EGSR 2026) |
| **[GC-MoE on SM120: Overlapping Expert-Parallel Token Dispatch with Warp-Level FP8xFP4 Block-Scaled MMA on Consumer Blackwell](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16219v1)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Expert-parallel Mixture-of-Experts decoding is latency bound: each layer serializes a token exchange, a grouped GEMM over routed experts, and a combine, while only a few tokens per rank are in flight. Prior GC-MoE fused these phases into one persistent, Green-Context-partitioned CUDA Graph on Hopper (SM90) and datacenter Blackwell (SM100). This paper rebuilds the operator for SM120 (compute capability 12.0, e.g. RTX PRO 5000) over PCIe. SM120 exposes block-scaled tensor cores only through warp-level mma.sync.aligned.kind::mxf8f6f4: no tensor memory, no two-CTA MMA, no cluster multicast. We rebuild the kernel around a swap-AB warp-level block-scaled MMA with FP4 weights in the register A operand and FP8 activations in B, plus SM120-specific mechanisms: scale-byte selection through MMA immediates, a compact-weights path, an adaptive token-tile geometry, an expert-granular streaming dispatch with a batched bulk-pull engine and a 64-bit deduplicating pull cache, full-width BF16 returns drained by resident relay copiers over a position-major arena, and shared experts folded into the compute role. On 8xRTX PRO 5000 over PCIe the operator is 1.522x and 1.387x faster than the FlashInfer CuTe DSL W4A8 MegaMoE and the Cake MegaMoE across 18 decode points (18/18 wins each), and 1.388x faster than Cake with shared experts fused; all 94 numerical cases and 752 per-rank checks pass, at cross-operator cosine similarity of at least 0.9999997.</p></details> | 24 pages, 10 tables, 6 figures |

Evaluation spans 7 model families (up to 70B). Without fine-tuning, data-free LILA surpasses PruneNet (45M-parameter RL policy) by up to 2.12~pp in zero-shot accuracy on LLaMA-2-7B and outperforms WikiText-2-calibrated SliceGPT by up to 6.1~pp across all sparsity levels, confirming data-free performance is not an artifact of omitting activation statistics. After one LoRA RFT epoch (matched WikiText-2 protocol), LILA Act surpasses SliceGPT+RFT by 6.39~pp on average using 98.4\% fewer calibration samples (16 vs.\ 1,024) across LLaMA-2-7B and Phi-2. NTK analysis confirms a $17{\times}$ reduction in functional distortion versus random pruning, providing theoretical grounding for the spectral criterion.</p></details> | 31 Pages |

| **[CD-RCM: Generalizable Continuous-Depth Novel View Synthesis for Reflectance Confocal Microscopy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12635v2)** | 2026-11-10 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Reflectance confocal microscopy (RCM) provides noninvasive, cellular-resolution "optical biopsies" of human skin in vivo by acquiring en-face images at successive depths, forming a sparse z-stack.
