Across diverse high-resolution datasets-from sparse segmentation masks to dense real-world photographs-TRUCO achieves speedups of 4.7x to 17.4x over the fastest sequential baseline with hierarchy computation, and 2.3x to 17.8x without it.</p></details> |  |
| **[OctreeSplat: Hierarchical Octree-Guided Feed-Forward Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02658v2)** | 2026-11-15 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Recent feed-forward 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) methods typically regress pixel-aligned Gaussian primitives, often causing excessive overlap and artifacts, while inaccuracies in predicted camera poses can lead to misalignment in novel-view synthesis (NVS). We present VoxelTTO, a feed-forward framework for reconstructing geometrically accurate 3DGS from an arbitrary number of images and optional camera parameters. VoxelTTO aggregates dense image features into global voxels and decodes Gaussians from voxel features, breaking the pixel-to-Gaussian correspondence. To exploit known camera parameters while keeping the pretrained visual foundation model (VFM) parameters frozen, we introduce test-time optimization (TTO) that adapts lightweight LoRA modules using pose supervision. We further replace vanilla 3DGS rasterization with stochastic solid volume rendering during training and inference, improving geometric fidelity. Training updates only the voxel-aligned Gaussian reconstruction modules, requiring 80 GPU hours. Experiments on Replica, Tanks and Temples, RE10K, and DTU demonstrate improved RGB-D NVS and camera-pose alignment relative to prior methods.</p></details> |  |
| **[GC-MoE on SM120: Overlapping Expert-Parallel Token Dispatch with Warp-Level FP8xFP4 Block-Scaled MMA on Consumer Blackwell](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16219v1)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Expert-parallel Mixture-of-Experts decoding is latency bound: each layer serializes a token exchange, a grouped GEMM over routed experts, and a combine, while only a few tokens per rank are in flight. Prior GC-MoE fused these phases into one persistent, Green-Context-partitioned CUDA Graph on Hopper (SM90) and datacenter Blackwell (SM100). This paper rebuilds the operator for SM120 (compute capability 12.0, e.g. RTX PRO 5000) over PCIe. SM120 exposes block-scaled tensor cores only through warp-level mma.sync.aligned.kind::mxf8f6f4: no tensor memory, no two-CTA MMA, no cluster multicast. We rebuild the kernel around a swap-AB warp-level block-scaled MMA with FP4 weights in the register A operand and FP8 activations in B, plus SM120-specific mechanisms: scale-byte selection through MMA immediates, a compact-weights path, an adaptive token-tile geometry, an expert-granular streaming dispatch with a batched bulk-pull engine and a 64-bit deduplicating pull cache, full-width BF16 returns drained by resident relay copiers over a position-major arena, and shared experts folded into the compute role. On 8xRTX PRO 5000 over PCIe the operator is 1.522x and 1.387x faster than the FlashInfer CuTe DSL W4A8 MegaMoE and the Cake MegaMoE across 18 decode points (18/18 wins each), and 1.388x faster than Cake with shared experts fused; all 94 numerical cases and 752 per-rank checks pass, at cross-operator cosine similarity of at least 0.9999997.</p></details> | 24 pages, 10 tables, 6 figures |
| **[LILA: Data-Free Structured Pruning of LLMs via Latent Spectral Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11163v2)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Structured pruning of large language models (LLMs) has universally required calibration data, gradient computation, or large auxiliary policy networks at pruning time. LILA (\emph{Latent-Informed Layer Analysis}) demonstrates that this dependency is avoidable: each FFN weight matrix is factorized with Non-negative Matrix Factorization (NMF) to obtain a parts-based spectral representation, and neuron importance is scored via the Kolmogorov--Smirnov (KS) distance between singular value distributions of the full and neuron-ablated NMF matrix, a closed-form criterion requiring no calibration corpus, forward pass, or gradient computation. Two data-free matrix variants (Abs, Split) generate pruning masks from weight spectral geometry alone; a calibration-guided variant (Act) is provided as an internal ablation. Evaluation spans 7 model families (up to 70B). Without fine-tuning, data-free LILA surpasses PruneNet (45M-parameter RL policy) by up to 2.12~pp in zero-shot accuracy on LLaMA-2-7B and outperforms WikiText-2-calibrated SliceGPT by up to 6.1~pp across all sparsity levels, confirming data-free performance is not an artifact of omitting activation statistics. After one LoRA RFT epoch (matched WikiText-2 protocol), LILA Act surpasses SliceGPT+RFT by 6.39~pp on average using 98.4\% fewer calibration samples (16 vs.\ 1,024) across LLaMA-2-7B and Phi-2. NTK analysis confirms a $17{\times}$ reduction in functional distortion versus random pruning, providing theoretical grounding for the spectral criterion.</p></details> | 31 Pages |
| **[ThermalSplat: Generalizable Pose-Free Gaussian Splatting for Sparse-View Thermal 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15435v1)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Thermal infrared imaging is vital for applications like search-and-rescue and autonomous navigation, yet trans- lating it into 3D scene representations remains a critical bottleneck. The low-texture nature and view-dependent emissivity of thermal images cause standard pose estima- tors to fail catastrophically, collapsing standard 3D Gaus- sian Splatting pipelines under sparse-view constraints. Fur- thermore, existing thermal reconstruction methods rely heavily on dense capture and computationally expensive per-scene optimization. Critically, many of these ap- proaches depend on aligned RGB-thermal datasets to ob- tain reliable camera poses and initial point clouds, ren- dering them unusable in purely thermal, unposed envi- ronments. To break this bottleneck, we present Thermal- Splat, the first generalizable, feed-forward Gaussian splat- ting framework that reconstructs 3D scenes directly from sparse thermal images without requiring known camera poses, RGB guidance, or per-scene training. We over- come thermal-specific challenges using a soft census loss for brightness-invariant structural matching and a bound- ary structure loss to explicitly supervise temperature discon- tinuities. Trained on an aggregated multi-source dataset, ThermalSplat synthesizes sharp reconstructions of unseen scenes in a single feed-forward pass. Extensive experi- ments demonstrate that ThermalSplat holds a strict advan- tage over scene-specific thermal splatting methods in the sparse regime. Furthermore, even compared against these baselines operating under their ideal dense capture set- tings, ThermalSplat achieves superior perceptual fidelity. By eliminating the need for computationally expensive per scene optimization, ThermalSplat offers an efficient and generalizable approach to thermal 3D reconstruction.</p></details> | WACV 2027 |
| **[A Multi-Parent Edge Assembly Crossover Driven Hybrid Genetic Algorithm for the Hamiltonian \textit{p}-Median Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15219v1)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The Hamiltonian \textit{p}-median problem seeks to partition the vertices of a graph into exactly $p$ disjoint cycles while minimizing their total cost. This paper presents a hybrid genetic algorithm incorporating a multi-parent edge assembly crossover operator. The crossover combines a base solution with multiple donor solutions in a single recombination operation, thereby expanding the space of available edge exchanges. It uses virtual copies of the base solution to establish edge balance, together with an auxiliary directed graph and a budgeted search to construct valid AB-cycles. Extensive experiments on 215 benchmark instances across four sets show that the proposed algorithm improves the best-known solution values for 34 instances and matches those for another 168. Additional experiments indicate that a moderate number of parents provides beneficial diversity, whereas excessive parent counts, particularly when $p$ is large, lead to excessive population dispersion and hinder convergence.</p></details> |  |
| **[An exact compress-with-another threshold in a binary automaton family](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07044v2)** | 2026-11-10 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>For a state $q$ in a synchronizing automaton, let $μ(q)$ be the minimum length of a word merging $q$ with another initial state. Szykuła asked whether $μ(q)=O(n)$ for strongly connected synchronizing automata and described a binary family attributed to Dżyga, with $n=3p$ states and a conjectured threshold $4n/3$. We specify the boundary transitions omitted from the published piecewise definition and prove that the resulting automata $\mathcal A_p$ satisfy $μ(q_0)=4p=4n/3$ for all $p\geq3$. A shortest word merging $q_0$ with another state is $(ba)^p(ab)^p$. We also construct a reset word of length $3p^2+4p-1$. Consequently, any uniform linear upper bound for $μ(q)$ on binary strongly connected synchronizing automata with corank-one letters has leading constant at least $4/3$.</p></details> | 9 pages, 1 figure. Revised proofs and exposition, corrected boundary transitions, updated references, and added a coauthor |
| **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset comprises 770 RANS simulations of 194 unique wing configurations, sampled across 27 airfoil profile variants and 4 distances from the ground, spanning variations in wing sweep, chord, taper, angle of attack, and tip twist. Six neural network architectures representing distinct paradigms --- convolutional (DoMINO), graph-based (X-MeshGraphNet), transformer-based (AB-UPT, GeoTransolver, GAOT), and JEPA-based (AeroJEPA) --- are evaluated on their ability to predict surface pressure coefficient and wall shear stress distributions, from which integral drag and lift coefficients are recovered. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |
| **[UltraVoxelGS: Voxel-First Feed-Forward Gaussian Splatting for 3D Ultrasound Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11951v1)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present UltraVoxelGS, a 3D ultrasound reconstruction method that achieves both fast reconstruction and physically faithful rendering for the first time, bringing live spatial feedback during 2D ultrasound scanning within reach. This is made possible by a feed-forward design that predicts a complete Gaussian scene representation from posed ultrasound images directly, entirely bypassing the per-scene optimization bottleneck that has confined prior methods to offline use. Realising such feed-forward prediction in this setting is non-trivial: unlike natural images, ultrasound slices share at most one-dimensional intersections and exhibit view-dependent appearance due to acoustic propagation and attenuation, violating the dense overlap and photometric consistency assumed by existing feed-forward Gaussian methods. Hence, to achieve fast reconstruction, we introduce a feed-forward voxel-to-Gaussian pass that decouples representation size from input count by lifting all posed slices into a fixed-size voxel support and predicting Gaussian parameters directly. For faithful rendering, we propose ultrasound-aware appearance rendering strategy factorize reflection from cumulative attenuation during training and exploit appearance continuity among spatially adjacent slices for efficient Render-time Appearance Adaptation. On both real-world and simulated datasets, UltraVoxelGS achieves best PSNR/SSIM compared with per-scene optimization baselines while reducing inference from 10-20 minutes to approximately 10 seconds (over 35 times faster) and scales to sequences exceeding 1,000 frames. Code is available at https://github.com/YuebyYue/UltraVoxelGS-camera-ready.</p></details> |  |

## pose-free 3D reconstruction
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v3)** | 2026-11-15 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extracting binary contours and their containment hierarchy is a fundamental step in many computer vision pipelines. While the standard Suzuki--Abe algorithm is inherently sequential, existing parallel tracers neither preserve its exact contours nor compute their hierarchy. This paper presents TRUCO (Threaded Raster Unrestricted Contour Ownership), a lock-free parallel algorithm for multi-core CPUs that produces the exact output of Suzuki--Abe. TRUCO relies on two core ideas. First, the image is partitioned into horizontal stripes, and a geometric ownership rule allows each thread to trace complete contours independently across stripe boundaries, avoiding both locks and post-processing merges. Second, contour tracing is decoupled from hierarchy reconstruction, which is performed in a subsequent parallel pass and can be skipped entirely when only the contours are needed. We formally prove that TRUCO is deterministic, data-race-free, and identical in output to Suzuki--Abe regardless of thread count. Across diverse high-resolution datasets-from sparse segmentation masks to dense real-world photographs-TRUCO achieves speedups of 4.7x to 17.4x over the fastest sequential baseline with hierarchy computation, and 2.3x to 17.8x without it.</p></details> |  |

| **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. | **[Active Learning for Gaussian Process Regression Under Self-Induced Boltzmann Weights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10654v2)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We consider the active learning problem where the goal is to learn an unknown function with low prediction error under an unknown Boltzmann distribution induced by the function itself. This self-induced weighting arises naturally in problems such as potential energy surface (PES) modeling in computational chemistry, yet poses unique challenges as the target distribution is unknown and its partition function is intractable. We propose \texttt{AB-SID-iVAR}, a Gaussian Process-based acquisition function that approximates the intractable Bayesian target distribution in closed form while avoiding partition function estimation, and is applicable to both discrete and continuous input domains. We also analyze a Thompson sampling alternative (\texttt{TS-SID-iVAR}) as a higher variance Monte Carlo variant. Despite the unknown target, under mild conditions, we establish that the terminal prediction error vanishes with high probability, and provide a tighter average-case guarantee. We demonstrate consistent improvements over existing approaches in this setting on synthetic benchmarks and real-world PES modeling and drug discovery tasks. Our code is available at https://github.com/TsingQAQ/SIDAL</p></details> |  |

| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v3)** | 2026-11-15 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extracting binary contours and their containment hierarchy is a fundamental step in many computer vision pipelines. While the standard Suzuki--Abe algorithm is inherently sequential, existing parallel tracers neither preserve its exact contours nor compute their hierarchy. This paper presents TRUCO (Threaded Raster Unrestricted Contour Ownership), a lock-free parallel algorithm for multi-core CPUs that produces the exact output of Suzuki--Abe. TRUCO relies on two core ideas. First, the image is partitioned into horizontal stripes, and a geometric ownership rule allows each thread to trace complete contours independently across stripe boundaries, avoiding both locks and post-processing merges. Second, contour tracing is decoupled from hierarchy reconstruction, which is performed in a subsequent parallel pass and can be skipped entirely when only the contours are needed. We formally prove that TRUCO is deterministic, data-race-free, and identical in output to Suzuki--Abe regardless of thread count. Across diverse high-resolution datasets-from sparse segmentation masks to dense real-world photographs-TRUCO achieves speedups of 4.7x to 17.4x over the fastest sequential baseline with hierarchy computation, and 2.3x to 17.8x without it.</p></details> |  |
| **[GC-MoE on SM120: Overlapping Expert-Parallel Token Dispatch with Warp-Level FP8xFP4 Block-Scaled MMA on Consumer Blackwell](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16219v1)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Expert-parallel Mixture-of-Experts decoding is latency bound: each layer serializes a token exchange, a grouped GEMM over routed experts, and a combine, while only a few tokens per rank are in flight. Prior GC-MoE fused these phases into one persistent, Green-Context-partitioned CUDA Graph on Hopper (SM90) and datacenter Blackwell (SM100). This paper rebuilds the operator for SM120 (compute capability 12.0, e.g. RTX PRO 5000) over PCIe. SM120 exposes block-scaled tensor cores only through warp-level mma.sync.aligned.kind::mxf8f6f4: no tensor memory, no two-CTA MMA, no cluster multicast. We rebuild the kernel around a swap-AB warp-level block-scaled MMA with FP4 weights in the register A operand and FP8 activations in B, plus SM120-specific mechanisms: scale-byte selection through MMA immediates, a compact-weights path, an adaptive token-tile geometry, an expert-granular streaming dispatch with a batched bulk-pull engine and a 64-bit deduplicating pull cache, full-width BF16 returns drained by resident relay copiers over a position-major arena, and shared experts folded into the compute role. On 8xRTX PRO 5000 over PCIe the operator is 1.522x and 1.387x faster than the FlashInfer CuTe DSL W4A8 MegaMoE and the Cake MegaMoE across 18 decode points (18/18 wins each), and 1.388x faster than Cake with shared experts fused; all 94 numerical cases and 752 per-rank checks pass, at cross-operator cosine similarity of at least 0.9999997.</p></details> | 24 pages, 10 tables, 6 figures |
| **[LILA: Data-Free Structured Pruning of LLMs via Latent Spectral Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11163v2)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Structured pruning of large language models (LLMs) has universally required calibration data, gradient computation, or large auxiliary policy networks at pruning time. LILA (\emph{Latent-Informed Layer Analysis}) demonstrates that this dependency is avoidable: each FFN weight matrix is factorized with Non-negative Matrix Factorization (NMF) to obtain a parts-based spectral representation, and neuron importance is scored via the Kolmogorov--Smirnov (KS) distance between singular value distributions of the full and neuron-ablated NMF matrix, a closed-form criterion requiring no calibration corpus, forward pass, or gradient computation. Two data-free matrix variants (Abs, Split) generate pruning masks from weight spectral geometry alone; a calibration-guided variant (Act) is provided as an internal ablation. Evaluation spans 7 model families (up to 70B). Without fine-tuning, data-free LILA surpasses PruneNet (45M-parameter RL policy) by up to 2.12~pp in zero-shot accuracy on LLaMA-2-7B and outperforms WikiText-2-calibrated SliceGPT by up to 6.1~pp across all sparsity levels, confirming data-free performance is not an artifact of omitting activation statistics. After one LoRA RFT epoch (matched WikiText-2 protocol), LILA Act surpasses SliceGPT+RFT by 6.39~pp on average using 98.4\% fewer calibration samples (16 vs.\ 1,024) across LLaMA-2-7B and Phi-2. NTK analysis confirms a $17{\times}$ reduction in functional distortion versus random pruning, providing theoretical grounding for the spectral criterion.</p></details> | 31 Pages |
| **[A Multi-Parent Edge Assembly Crossover Driven Hybrid Genetic Algorithm for the Hamiltonian \textit{p}-Median Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15219v1)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The Hamiltonian \textit{p}-median problem seeks to partition the vertices of a graph into exactly $p$ disjoint cycles while minimizing their total cost. This paper presents a hybrid genetic algorithm incorporating a multi-parent edge assembly crossover operator. The crossover combines a base solution with multiple donor solutions in a single recombination operation, thereby expanding the space of available edge exchanges. It uses virtual copies of the base solution to establish edge balance, together with an auxiliary directed graph and a budgeted search to construct valid AB-cycles. Extensive experiments on 215 benchmark instances across four sets show that the proposed algorithm improves the best-known solution values for 34 instances and matches those for another 168. Additional experiments indicate that a moderate number of parents provides beneficial diversity, whereas excessive parent counts, particularly when $p$ is large, lead to excessive population dispersion and hinder convergence.</p></details> |  |
| **[An exact compress-with-another threshold in a binary automaton family](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07044v2)** | 2026-11-10 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>For a state $q$ in a synchronizing automaton, let $μ(q)$ be the minimum length of a word merging $q$ with another initial state. Szykuła asked whether $μ(q)=O(n)$ for strongly connected synchronizing automata and described a binary family attributed to Dżyga, with $n=3p$ states and a conjectured threshold $4n/3$. We specify the boundary transitions omitted from the published piecewise definition and prove that the resulting automata $\mathcal A_p$ satisfy $μ(q_0)=4p=4n/3$ for all $p\geq3$. A shortest word merging $q_0$ with another state is $(ba)^p(ab)^p$. We also construct a reset word of length $3p^2+4p-1$. Consequently, any uniform linear upper bound for $μ(q)$ on binary strongly connected synchronizing automata with corank-one letters has leading constant at least $4/3$.</p></details> | 9 pages, 1 figure. Revised proofs and exposition, corrected boundary transitions, updated references, and added a coauthor |
| **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset comprises 770 RANS simulations of 194 unique wing configurations, sampled across 27 airfoil profile variants and 4 distances from the ground, spanning variations in wing sweep, chord, taper, angle of attack, and tip twist. Six neural network architectures representing distinct paradigms --- convolutional (DoMINO), graph-based (X-MeshGraphNet), transformer-based (AB-UPT, GeoTransolver, GAOT), and JEPA-based (AeroJEPA) --- are evaluated on their ability to predict surface pressure coefficient and wall shear stress distributions, from which integral drag and lift coefficients are recovered. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |
| **[Active Learning for Gaussian Process Regression Under Self-Induced Boltzmann Weights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10654v2)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We consider the active learning problem where the goal is to learn an unknown function with low prediction error under an unknown Boltzmann distribution induced by the function itself. This self-induced weighting arises naturally in problems such as potential energy surface (PES) modeling in computational chemistry, yet poses unique challenges as the target distribution is unknown and its partition function is intractable. We propose \texttt{AB-SID-iVAR}, a Gaussian Process-based acquisition function that approximates the intractable Bayesian target distribution in closed form while avoiding partition function estimation, and is applicable to both discrete and continuous input domains. We also analyze a Thompson sampling alternative (\texttt{TS-SID-iVAR}) as a higher variance Monte Carlo variant. Despite the unknown target, under mild conditions, we establish that the terminal prediction error vanishes with high probability, and provide a tighter average-case guarantee. We demonstrate consistent improvements over existing approaches in this setting on synthetic benchmarks and real-world PES modeling and drug discovery tasks. Our code is available at https://github.com/TsingQAQ/SIDAL</p></details> |  |

## dynamic Gaussian Splatting
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[CueSplat: Learning to Schedule Sparse Deformation for Dynamic Gaussian Splatting on Edge Devices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22095v1)** | 2026-11-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Deformation-based 4D Gaussian splatting (4DGS) enables dynamic scene rendering. However, evaluating deformation networks for all Gaussians at every frame is compute-intensive on edge devices. We find that Gaussian trajectories are often locally predictable, yet prediction reliability varies across Gaussians and time. Thus, we propose CueSplat, a post-training framework that exploits local temporal regularity while keeping the pretrained backbone frozen and retaining its Gaussian set. Specifically, CueSplat predicts Gaussian attributes from recent history and uses a lightweight learned router to select exact deformation updates within a scene-specific budget calibrated offline. Selection accounts for prediction reliability and rendering contribution. Selected indices are reused across nearby frames to reduce routing overhead, while selected Gaussians still receive exact updates each frame. To control accumulated prediction error, we use drift signals to schedule three consecutive full-deformation frames that rebuild the prediction history. Evaluated across four mainstream 4DGS algorithms, CueSplat achieves up to $6.74\times$ and $8.15\times$ speedups on Jetson AGX Orin and Orin NX, respectively, with a negligible quality drop (at most $0.16$ dB in dataset-average PSNR).</p></details> | 10 pages, 6 figures. Main paper only |

## Gaussian Splatting SLAM
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[CoSphere-SLAM: Multi-Agent Panoramic Gaussian Splatting SLAM with Spherical Loop Closure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22235v1)** | 2026-11-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Dense collaborative mapping is essential for search and rescue, building inspection, and warehouse automation, where panoramic cameras capture the entire surroundings in a single frame. Unlike pinhole agents that share views only at similar headings, panoramic agents observe overlapping scenes at any 3D orientation, yet searching full rotations also produces mutually consistent false loop closures across distinct symmetric rooms. Moreover, continuous monocular scale drift splits rigidly merged maps at revisited surfaces, while depth noise at short baselines distorts loop translation directions. To address these challenges, we propose CoSphere-SLAM, the first multi-agent Gaussian splatting SLAM framework for panoramic cameras, whose spherical loop closure aligns spherical features over $\mathrm{SO}(3)$ via fast Fourier correlation across arbitrary orientations and rejects symmetric false closures through global $\mathrm{Sim}(3)$ consensus. Building on these closures, directional global optimization solves a per-keyframe $\mathrm{Sim}(3)$ pose graph with log-scale regularization to correct scale drift while separating optical-flow translation directions from depth-based magnitudes. Finally, omnidirectional Gaussian fusion transforms each Gaussian with its keyframe similarity and renders Gaussians at their maximum ray response to jointly refine poses and maps. Experiments on indoor and cross-session multi-agent panoramic benchmarks demonstrate that CoSphere-SLAM achieves state-of-the-art trajectory accuracy and rendering quality among single-agent and multi-agent methods. Project website: https://cosphere-slam.github.io.</p></details> | 9 pages, 4 figures, 7 tables. Project website: https://cosphere-slam.github.io |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v3)** | 2026-11-15 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extracting binary contours and their containment hierarchy is a fundamental step in many computer vision pipelines. We formally prove that TRUCO is deterministic, data-race-free, and identical in output to Suzuki--Abe regardless of thread count. Across diverse high-resolution datasets-from sparse segmentation masks to dense real-world photographs-TRUCO achieves speedups of 4.7x to 17.4x over the fastest sequential baseline with hierarchy computation, and 2.3x to 17.8x without it.</p></details> |  |
| **[DPGS-SLAM: Diffusion-Prior and Provenance-Aware Monocular Gaussian Splatting SLAM for Dynamic Visual Sensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12038v2)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents DPGS-SLAM (Diffusion-Prior and Provenance-Aware Monocular Gaussian Splatting SLAM) for dense mapping in environments containing dynamic objects. Objects in dynamic motion remain a significant challenge for dense visual SLAM since dynamic observations can contaminate camera tracking and scene reconstruction. Although 3DGS (3D Gaussian splatting) enables efficient dense mapping, existing 3DGS-SLAM systems may exhibit instability when dynamic objects violate their underlying static-scene assumption. Excluding dynamic observations can reduce their influence on tracking and mapping, but may leave occluded static backgrounds missing or weakly constrained. Our proposed DPGS-SLAM is a monocular RGB SLAM framework that supports provisional representations of insufficiently observed static backgrounds through asynchronous diffusion-based background completion and provenance-aware Gaussian mapping. Dynamic regions are identified through optical-flow-based motion discovery and temporally consistent mask propagation by SAM2. For selected keyframes, a fine-tuned diffusion inpainting model generates plausible background appearance in occluded regions, while metric depth predicted from completed images initializes provisional Gaussian primitives. Provenance labels distinguish generated primitives from observation-supported primitives, excluding generated content from camera-pose estimation and observed-map optimization. When previously occluded backgrounds become visible, eligible generated primitives are removed, and observation-supported primitives are independently initialized from real RGB observations and predicted depth. Experimental results demonstrated competitive camera tracking, with average ATE RMSE values of 1.2 cm on TUM RGB-D and 2.7 cm on BONN RGB-D datasets. On StaDy4D, DPGS-SLAM improved average PSNR by 4.60 dB over WildGS-SLAM using only monocular RGB input.</p></details> |  |
| **[GC-MoE on SM120: Overlapping Expert-Parallel Token Dispatch with Warp-Level FP8xFP4 Block-Scaled MMA on Consumer Blackwell](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16219v1)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Expert-parallel Mixture-of-Experts decoding is latency bound: each layer serializes a token exchange, a grouped GEMM over routed experts, and a combine, while only a few tokens per rank are in flight. Prior GC-MoE fused these phases into one persistent, Green-Context-partitioned CUDA Graph on Hopper (SM90) and datacenter Blackwell (SM100). This paper rebuilds the operator for SM120 (compute capability 12.0, e.g. RTX PRO 5000) over PCIe. SM120 exposes block-scaled tensor cores only through warp-level mma.sync.aligned.kind::mxf8f6f4: no tensor memory, no two-CTA MMA, no cluster multicast. We rebuild the kernel around a swap-AB warp-level block-scaled MMA with FP4 weights in the register A operand and FP8 activations in B, plus SM120-specific mechanisms: scale-byte selection through MMA immediates, a compact-weights path, an adaptive token-tile geometry, an expert-granular streaming dispatch with a batched bulk-pull engine and a 64-bit deduplicating pull cache, full-width BF16 returns drained by resident relay copiers over a position-major arena, and shared experts folded into the compute role. On 8xRTX PRO 5000 over PCIe the operator is 1.522x and 1.387x faster than the FlashInfer CuTe DSL W4A8 MegaMoE and the Cake MegaMoE across 18 decode points (18/18 wins each), and 1.388x faster than Cake with shared experts fused; all 94 numerical cases and 752 per-rank checks pass, at cross-operator cosine similarity of at least 0.9999997.</p></details> | 24 pages, 10 tables, 6 figures |
| **[LILA: Data-Free Structured Pruning of LLMs via Latent Spectral Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11163v2)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Structured pruning of large language models (LLMs) has universally required calibration data, gradient computation, or large auxiliary policy networks at pruning time. LILA (\emph{Latent-Informed Layer Analysis}) demonstrates that this dependency is avoidable: each FFN weight matrix is factorized with Non-negative Matrix Factorization (NMF) to obtain a parts-based spectral representation, and neuron importance is scored via the Kolmogorov--Smirnov (KS) distance between singular value distributions of the full and neuron-ablated NMF matrix, a closed-form criterion requiring no calibration corpus, forward pass, or gradient computation. Two data-free matrix variants (Abs, Split) generate pruning masks from weight spectral geometry alone; a calibration-guided variant (Act) is provided as an internal ablation. Evaluation spans 7 model families (up to 70B). Without fine-tuning, data-free LILA surpasses PruneNet (45M-parameter RL policy) by up to 2.12~pp in zero-shot accuracy on LLaMA-2-7B and outperforms WikiText-2-calibrated SliceGPT by up to 6.1~pp across all sparsity levels, confirming data-free performance is not an artifact of omitting activation statistics. After one LoRA RFT epoch (matched WikiText-2 protocol), LILA Act surpasses SliceGPT+RFT by 6.39~pp on average using 98.4\% fewer calibration samples (16 vs.\ 1,024) across LLaMA-2-7B and Phi-2. NTK analysis confirms a $17{\times}$ reduction in functional distortion versus random pruning, providing theoretical grounding for the spectral criterion.</p></details> | 31 Pages |
| **[A Multi-Parent Edge Assembly Crossover Driven Hybrid Genetic Algorithm for the Hamiltonian \textit{p}-Median Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15219v1)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The Hamiltonian \textit{p}-median problem seeks to partition the vertices of a graph into exactly $p$ disjoint cycles while minimizing their total cost. This paper presents a hybrid genetic algorithm incorporating a multi-parent edge assembly crossover operator. The crossover combines a base solution with multiple donor solutions in a single recombination operation, thereby expanding the space of available edge exchanges. It uses virtual copies of the base solution to establish edge balance, together with an auxiliary directed graph and a budgeted search to construct valid AB-cycles. Extensive experiments on 215 benchmark instances across four sets show that the proposed algorithm improves the best-known solution values for 34 instances and matches those for another 168. Additional experiments indicate that a moderate number of parents provides beneficial diversity, whereas excessive parent counts, particularly when $p$ is large, lead to excessive population dispersion and hinder convergence.</p></details> |  |
| **[An exact compress-with-another threshold in a binary automaton family](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07044v2)** | 2026-11-10 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>For a state $q$ in a synchronizing automaton, let $μ(q)$ be the minimum length of a word merging $q$ with another initial state. Szykuła asked whether $μ(q)=O(n)$ for strongly connected synchronizing automata and described a binary family attributed to Dżyga, with $n=3p$ states and a conjectured threshold $4n/3$. We specify the boundary transitions omitted from the published piecewise definition and prove that the resulting automata $\mathcal A_p$ satisfy $μ(q_0)=4p=4n/3$ for all $p\geq3$. A shortest word merging $q_0$ with another state is $(ba)^p(ab)^p$. We also construct a reset word of length $3p^2+4p-1$. Consequently, any uniform linear upper bound for $μ(q)$ on binary strongly connected synchronizing automata with corank-one letters has leading constant at least $4/3$.</p></details> | 9 pages, 1 figure. Revised proofs and exposition, corrected boundary transitions, updated references, and added a coauthor |
| **[GEMOR-GS: Global Extrinsic-calibration-refined Mapping and Online Relocalization for 3D Gaussian-Splatting SLAM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13528v1)** | 2026-11-10 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Photorealistic and metrically consistent world representations are a primary requirement for LiDAR--inertial--visual SLAM systems that serve robots over continuous operation. 3D Gaussian splatting makes such representations attainable by supplying an explicit, renderable, and queryable scene model. We present GEMOR-GS (Global Extrinsic-calibration-refined Mapping and Online Relocalization for 3D Gaussian-Splatting SLAM) to make the Gaussian map geometrically accurate, globally refined, and re-enterable, providing a better appearance for robot perception and decision-making. To improve the 3DGS map, the LiDAR--camera extrinsic is first estimated offline by direct-method alignment over dedicated calibration segments and reused by all following online and offline processes. Under the calibrated geometry, keyframe poses and Gaussian parameters are refined alternately under a gated differentiable-rendering and LiDAR--projection objective. The final global reconstruction is then rebuilt from the refined keyframe schema with all online constraints lifted. For the robot SLAM lifecycle, the method recovers the pose from the 3DGS map after tracking loss through an XFeat bypass map, RANSAC-PnP coarse localization, and Gaussian RGB-D warp refinement. Experiments on one self-collected and five public sequences quantify each element: the offline reconstruction wins all paired keyframes against the online map by 14.3--19.9 dB, improves PSNR by approximately 9 dB over Gaussian-LIC2, and achieves 86--100% held-out relocalization success tied to extrinsic quality. The results show that GEMOR-GS improves the geometric accuracy, appearance fidelity, and re-enterability of the 3DGS map, serving as a dependable robot world model for SLAM and downstream tasks.</p></details> |  |
| **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset comprises 770 RANS simulations of 194 unique wing configurations, sampled across 27 airfoil profile variants and 4 distances from the ground, spanning variations in wing sweep, chord, taper, angle of attack, and tip twist. Six neural network architectures representing distinct paradigms --- convolutional (DoMINO), graph-based (X-MeshGraphNet), transformer-based (AB-UPT, GeoTransolver, GAOT), and JEPA-based (AeroJEPA) --- are evaluated on their ability to predict surface pressure coefficient and wall shear stress distributions, from which integral drag and lift coefficients are recovered. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |

| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v3)** | 2026-11-15 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extracting binary contours and their containment hierarchy is a fundamental step in many computer vision pipelines. | **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. | **[Active Learning for Gaussian Process Regression Under Self-Induced Boltzmann Weights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10654v2)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We consider the active learning problem where the goal is to learn an unknown function with low prediction error under an unknown Boltzmann distribution induced by the function itself. This self-induced weighting arises naturally in problems such as potential energy surface (PES) modeling in computational chemistry, yet poses unique challenges as the target distribution is unknown and its partition function is intractable. We propose \texttt{AB-SID-iVAR}, a Gaussian Process-based acquisition function that approximates the intractable Bayesian target distribution in closed form while avoiding partition function estimation, and is applicable to both discrete and continuous input domains. We also analyze a Thompson sampling alternative (\texttt{TS-SID-iVAR}) as a higher variance Monte Carlo variant. Despite the unknown target, under mild conditions, we establish that the terminal prediction error vanishes with high probability, and provide a tighter average-case guarantee. We demonstrate consistent improvements over existing approaches in this setting on synthetic benchmarks and real-world PES modeling and drug discovery tasks. Our code is available at https://github.com/TsingQAQ/SIDAL</p></details> |  |

| **[GC-MoE on SM120: Overlapping Expert-Parallel Token Dispatch with Warp-Level FP8xFP4 Block-Scaled MMA on Consumer Blackwell](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16219v1)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Expert-parallel Mixture-of-Experts decoding is latency bound: each layer serializes a token exchange, a grouped GEMM over routed experts, and a combine, while only a few tokens per rank are in flight. | **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. Six neural network architectures representing distinct paradigms --- convolutional (DoMINO), graph-based (X-MeshGraphNet), transformer-based (AB-UPT, GeoTransolver, GAOT), and JEPA-based (AeroJEPA) --- are evaluated on their ability to predict surface pressure coefficient and wall shear stress distributions, from which integral drag and lift coefficients are recovered. | **[Active Learning for Gaussian Process Regression Under Self-Induced Boltzmann Weights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10654v2)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We consider the active learning problem where the goal is to learn an unknown function with low prediction error under an unknown Boltzmann distribution induced by the function itself. We propose \texttt{AB-SID-iVAR}, a Gaussian Process-based acquisition function that approximates the intractable Bayesian target distribution in closed form while avoiding partition function estimation, and is applicable to both discrete and continuous input domains. Despite the unknown target, under mild conditions, we establish that the terminal prediction error vanishes with high probability, and provide a tighter average-case guarantee. We demonstrate consistent improvements over existing approaches in this setting on synthetic benchmarks and real-world PES modeling and drug discovery tasks. Our code is available at https://github.com/TsingQAQ/SIDAL</p></details> |  |

| **[QuantileGS: Quantile-Based Conformal Bounds on RGB Rendering Errors for Novel View Synthesis with 3D Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21253v1)** | 2026-11-16 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Uncertainty quantification for novel view synthesis with 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) assesses rendering reliability at unseen viewpoints. This reliability depends on the discrepancy between rendered and true colors, which cannot be measured without ground-truth images. We therefore quantify uncertainty in this unknown discrepancy through upper bounds on per-pixel RGB mean absolute error. However, learned bounds may underestimate these errors and overstate rendering reliability, motivating calibration to a prescribed coverage level. In this paper, we propose QuantileGS, a post-hoc framework that combines learned rendering-error quantiles with scale-normalized conformal calibration to produce spatially adaptive error bounds. The framework comprises three phases. Phase 1, Rendering-Error Computation, measures RGB mean absolute error between observed images and predictions from a frozen 3DGS model. Phase 2, Ordered Gaussian Quantile Fields, learns two scalar attributes per Gaussian to predict lower and upper rendering-error quantiles. Phase 3, Scale-Normalized Conformal Calibration, calibrates the upper prediction on held-out images, using the quantile gap to scale the correction. Under the stated assumptions, the resulting bounds provide finite-sample marginal coverage. At a nominal coverage level of 90%, calibration raises mean empirical test coverage from 69.6% to 89.6% on LLFF and from 80.1% to 92.1% on Mip-NeRF 360. On LF, coverage remains conservative at 96.3% before and 96.4% after calibration. Applications to active view selection, Gaussian pruning, and RGB-D simultaneous localization and mapping illustrate how these bounds inform downstream decisions.</p></details> |  |

| **[ThermalSplat: Generalizable Pose-Free Gaussian Splatting for Sparse-View Thermal 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15435v1)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Thermal infrared imaging is vital for applications like search-and-rescue and autonomous navigation, yet trans- lating it into 3D scene representations remains a critical bottleneck. The low-texture nature and view-dependent emissivity of thermal images cause standard pose estima- tors to fail catastrophically, collapsing standard 3D Gaus- sian Splatting pipelines under sparse-view constraints. Fur- thermore, existing thermal reconstruction methods rely heavily on dense capture and computationally expensive per-scene optimization. Critically, many of these ap- proaches depend on aligned RGB-thermal datasets to ob- tain reliable camera poses and initial point clouds, ren- dering them unusable in purely thermal, unposed envi- ronments. We over- come thermal-specific challenges using a soft census loss for brightness-invariant structural matching and a bound- ary structure loss to explicitly supervise temperature discon- tinuities. Trained on an aggregated multi-source dataset, ThermalSplat synthesizes sharp reconstructions of unseen scenes in a single feed-forward pass. Extensive experi- ments demonstrate that ThermalSplat holds a strict advan- tage over scene-specific thermal splatting methods in the sparse regime. Furthermore, even compared against these baselines operating under their ideal dense capture set- tings, ThermalSplat achieves superior perceptual fidelity. By eliminating the need for computationally expensive per scene optimization, ThermalSplat offers an efficient and generalizable approach to thermal 3D reconstruction.</p></details> | WACV 2027 |

On 8xRTX PRO 5000 over PCIe the operator is 1.522x and 1.387x faster than the FlashInfer CuTe DSL W4A8 MegaMoE and the Cake MegaMoE across 18 decode points (18/18 wins each), and 1.388x faster than Cake with shared experts fused; all 94 numerical cases and 752 per-rank checks pass, at cross-operator cosine similarity of at least 0.9999997.</p></details> | 24 pages, 10 tables, 6 figures |
| **[LILA: Data-Free Structured Pruning of LLMs via Latent Spectral Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11163v2)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Structured pruning of large language models (LLMs) has universally required calibration data, gradient computation, or large auxiliary policy networks at pruning time. LILA (\emph{Latent-Informed Layer Analysis}) demonstrates that this dependency is avoidable: each FFN weight matrix is factorized with Non-negative Matrix Factorization (NMF) to obtain a parts-based spectral representation, and neuron importance is scored via the Kolmogorov--Smirnov (KS) distance between singular value distributions of the full and neuron-ablated NMF matrix, a closed-form criterion requiring no calibration corpus, forward pass, or gradient computation. Two data-free matrix variants (Abs, Split) generate pruning masks from weight spectral geometry alone; a calibration-guided variant (Act) is provided as an internal ablation. Evaluation spans 7 model families (up to 70B). Without fine-tuning, data-free LILA surpasses PruneNet (45M-parameter RL policy) by up to 2.12~pp in zero-shot accuracy on LLaMA-2-7B and outperforms WikiText-2-calibrated SliceGPT by up to 6.1~pp across all sparsity levels, confirming data-free performance is not an artifact of omitting activation statistics. After one LoRA RFT epoch (matched WikiText-2 protocol), LILA Act surpasses SliceGPT+RFT by 6.39~pp on average using 98.4\% fewer calibration samples (16 vs.\ 1,024) across LLaMA-2-7B and Phi-2. NTK analysis confirms a $17{\times}$ reduction in functional distortion versus random pruning, providing theoretical grounding for the spectral criterion.</p></details> | 31 Pages |
| **[A Multi-Parent Edge Assembly Crossover Driven Hybrid Genetic Algorithm for the Hamiltonian \textit{p}-Median Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15219v1)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The Hamiltonian \textit{p}-median problem seeks to partition the vertices of a graph into exactly $p$ disjoint cycles while minimizing their total cost. This paper presents a hybrid genetic algorithm incorporating a multi-parent edge assembly crossover operator. The crossover combines a base solution with multiple donor solutions in a single recombination operation, thereby expanding the space of available edge exchanges. It uses virtual copies of the base solution to establish edge balance, together with an auxiliary directed graph and a budgeted search to construct valid AB-cycles. Extensive experiments on 215 benchmark instances across four sets show that the proposed algorithm improves the best-known solution values for 34 instances and matches those for another 168. Additional experiments indicate that a moderate number of parents provides beneficial diversity, whereas excessive parent counts, particularly when $p$ is large, lead to excessive population dispersion and hinder convergence.</p></details> |  |
| **[An exact compress-with-another threshold in a binary automaton family](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07044v2)** | 2026-11-10 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>For a state $q$ in a synchronizing automaton, let $μ(q)$ be the minimum length of a word merging $q$ with another initial state. Szykuła asked whether $μ(q)=O(n)$ for strongly connected synchronizing automata and described a binary family attributed to Dżyga, with $n=3p$ states and a conjectured threshold $4n/3$. We specify the boundary transitions omitted from the published piecewise definition and prove that the resulting automata $\mathcal A_p$ satisfy $μ(q_0)=4p=4n/3$ for all $p\geq3$. A shortest word merging $q_0$ with another state is $(ba)^p(ab)^p$. We also construct a reset word of length $3p^2+4p-1$. Consequently, any uniform linear upper bound for $μ(q)$ on binary strongly connected synchronizing automata with corank-one letters has leading constant at least $4/3$.</p></details> | 9 pages, 1 figure. Revised proofs and exposition, corrected boundary transitions, updated references, and added a coauthor |
| **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset comprises 770 RANS simulations of 194 unique wing configurations, sampled across 27 airfoil profile variants and 4 distances from the ground, spanning variations in wing sweep, chord, taper, angle of attack, and tip twist. Six neural network architectures representing distinct paradigms --- convolutional (DoMINO), graph-based (X-MeshGraphNet), transformer-based (AB-UPT, GeoTransolver, GAOT), and JEPA-based (AeroJEPA) --- are evaluated on their ability to predict surface pressure coefficient and wall shear stress distributions, from which integral drag and lift coefficients are recovered. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |
| **[Active Learning for Gaussian Process Regression Under Self-Induced Boltzmann Weights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10654v2)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We consider the active learning problem where the goal is to learn an unknown function with low prediction error under an unknown Boltzmann distribution induced by the function itself. This self-induced weighting arises naturally in problems such as potential energy surface (PES) modeling in computational chemistry, yet poses unique challenges as the target distribution is unknown and its partition function is intractable. We propose \texttt{AB-SID-iVAR}, a Gaussian Process-based acquisition function that approximates the intractable Bayesian target distribution in closed form while avoiding partition function estimation, and is applicable to both discrete and continuous input domains. We also analyze a Thompson sampling alternative (\texttt{TS-SID-iVAR}) as a higher variance Monte Carlo variant. Despite the unknown target, under mild conditions, we establish that the terminal prediction error vanishes with high probability, and provide a tighter average-case guarantee. We demonstrate consistent improvements over existing approaches in this setting on synthetic benchmarks and real-world PES modeling and drug discovery tasks. Our code is available at https://github.com/TsingQAQ/SIDAL</p></details> |  |
